/goal Generalize the channel-endpoint reference-counting facility into a user-facing OPT-IN deterministic reference-counted object kind (`@RefCounted`, Swift-ARC style), so any annotated type gets DETERMINISTIC `Drop` at strong-count 0 — promoting the deterministic-finalization carve-out that channel `Sender`/`Receiver` handles already use (see ROADMAP "deterministic endpoint release" + docs/16 §8) from a hardcoded runtime special-case into a real language feature. Implement at full Rust/Swift quality, no shortcuts: a refcounted-object header/descriptor variant carrying the strong count; ARC-style retain/release insertion across codegen (bind/copy/param/return/capture + heap stores via the docs/16 §7 write barriers); count→0 runs the type's `Drop` immediately as non-waiting cleanup then frees; and the tracing GC retained as the CYCLE-collector backstop (refcounting leaks cycles — the collector reclaims them, Python-style). Ask first before finalizing the surface syntax / any language-design decision (this is a genuine spec addition — add a new docs section and reconcile with docs/16 §8). [DONE] /goal Implement `await`/suspension in genuinely-conditional positions: the right operand of `&&`/`||` and the condition of a `while` loop (today these correctly error "await in this position is not yet supported" rather than miscompiling). [DONE] /goal Allow async closures in `Thread.spawn` (docs/20 §1, §4; docs/21 §6) — APPROVED design change: `Thread.spawn` accepts either an ordinary non-async `() => R` or an async `() => Future<R>` closure, and always returns `JoinHandle<R>` (joining on the AWAITED `R`). Implemented: new `lang_thread_spawn_async(env, pending_tid)` runtime entry (closure-call + private root-driver; earlier notes used the old internal `block_on` nickname, but the async-contract cleanup retired that nickname and the private ABI is now `lang_drive_root_future`); checker detects the async closure (return type `Future<T'>`) and yields `JoinHandle<T'>`; backend `emit_thread_spawn` calls the new entry (pending_tid, no float_kind) when async; the Shared-lock rejection is narrowed to ordinary non-async workers (async workers may await + lock); trailing `Thread.spawn { async => … }` parses; `JoinHandle.detach()` implemented (`lang_thread_detach`); a captured channel endpoint is owned by the future (released when the future resolves, not when the building closure returns) so a worker can await-then-send/recv across a suspension. Tests: runtime poll/resolution (immediately-ready + suspend/resume), compiler (async→JoinHandle<T'>, async worker CAN lock, ordinary non-async worker still errors, trailing form), parser (trailing async forms), LSP (hover), e2e concurrency (lock, parallel, detach, cross-thread channel, GC-stress) + native parity. Known limitation: one OS thread per async worker; worker-panic isolation remains separate.

[DONE] /goal Implement worker-panic isolation for threads and `spawn` workers (docs/20, docs/14): a panicking worker must fail only itself and surface as `Panicked{message}` to the joiner, NOT abort the whole process. — DONE: host unwinding can't cross Cranelift frames (no unwind tables), so each worker runs its body under a `setjmp`/`longjmp` PANIC BOUNDARY at its entry (`crates/runtime/src/panic_boundary.c`, built by `build.rs` via the `cc` crate; Rust glue in `panic_boundary.rs`). `lang_panic` checks `otter_pb_active()`: on a worker it captures the message and `longjmp`s back to the boundary (restoring the saved context directly across the generated frames — no frame-walk); on the MAIN thread no boundary is installed, so a top-level panic stays fatal (exit 101). The boundary (`run_under_boundary`) restores the invariants the longjmp skipped: drains held `Shared` locks (`lang_shared_release_all`, no poisoning) + the thread's transient cross-`poll` GC pins (new `gc::pin_for_unwind`/`unpin_for_unwind`/`release_unwind_pins`, used by the private root-future driver), then materialises the message as a pinned `str`. `finish_worker` publishes `Panicked{message}` so `JoinHandle.join()` surfaces it recoverably, while a `spawn EXPR` awaiter has the panic RE-PROPAGATED at its `await` (`spawn_poll`) per docs/21 §11. Covers `Thread.spawn` (ordinary non-async + async closures) and the `spawn` keyword. JIT + native parity (C shim bundled into both the rlib and libruntime.a). No language-design decision needed (behavior was already specified in docs/14 §7, docs/16 panic row, docs/20 §4, docs/21 §11). Tests: 5 runtime unit/integration (panic_boundary + real worker-panic with sibling survival) + 4 CLI integration (isolated join + message, lock-release-no-poison, `spawn` propagation exits non-zero, GC-stress parity) + e2e `concurrency/{worker_panic_sibling_survives,worker_panic_gc_stress,spawn_panic_propagates,lock_released_on_panic}` (last graduated from XFAIL); full workspace + 260-case e2e suite green, 0 XFAIL. The executor-multiplexed task-panic case is the SEPARATE follow-on goal below (depends on the M:N executor).

[DONE] /goal Resolve `@Variadic` extern functions (docs/19 §13), which was blocked because Cranelift lacks portable varargs-ABI support (aarch64 Apple passes varargs on the stack; Cranelift uses registers). — DONE via `libffi` (option A, user-approved). Confirmed Cranelift 0.132 has zero variadic support (`ir::Signature` = `{params, returns, call_conv}`, no fixed/variadic boundary, no `%al` mechanism), so a variadic call genuinely cannot be lowered as an ordinary call on the targets we emit (aarch64-apple-darwin stack passing, x86-64 SysV `%al`, Windows x64 float duplication). Implementation: the backend (`gen_hir::h_variadic_call`) marshals each argument — C-default-promoted in variadic position (`f32`→`f64`, sub-int/`bool`/`char`→`int`, `@Transparent` seen through to its inner scalar via `transparent_inner`), sign/zero-extended into a flat 8-byte-slot value buffer with a parallel type-tag array — gets the callee address via `func_addr`, and calls the runtime shim `lang_variadic_call` (`crates/runtime/src/variadic.rs`), a minimal hand binding to the *system* `libffi` (neither `pkg-config` nor autotools is available here, but `-lffi` links cleanly; `ffi_cif` is treated as an opaque over-sized buffer, the only target constant `FFI_DEFAULT_ABI` verified against ffitarget.h) that drives `ffi_prep_cif_var`/`ffi_call`. JIT links libffi via the runtime build script (`cargo:rustc-link-lib=ffi`); native `otter_fusion build` links `-lffi` when the program declares any `@Variadic`. Checker (`sema/check`): `validate_variadic` rejects `@Variadic` off an extern import / on an export-with-body / with no fixed prefix / with decorator args; `check_variadic_call` relaxes call arity to `>= n_fixed` and rejects non-passable variadic args (aggregates, `str` — pass a `*u8`). Used as a value (not called by name) the import is an ordinary non-variadic fn pointer over its fixed prefix (safe, no soundness hole). The LSP inherits the fix (shared `analyze_multi_ctx` checker). Verified against real `printf`/`snprintf`: int/str/double/char/hex, i64/u64 full width, f32→double promotion, negative sign-extension, `@Transparent` newtype, no-vararg path — JIT + native parity + GC-stress all identical. Tests: 2 runtime unit tests (drive `lang_variadic_call` against `snprintf` directly), 9 checker unit tests, 4 e2e `run` cases (`tests/cases/ffi/variadic_*`), 7 e2e compile-error cases (`tests/cases/errors/variadic_*`), 1 native-parity test (`run.rs`). Full workspace + 272-case e2e suite green. Docs (docs/19 §7 `@Variadic` subsection) and ROADMAP updated.

[DONE] /goal Add OS-backed `std:rand` RNG handles. — DONE: `std:rand` is now a mixed module: `SeededRng` remains the deterministic Otter-authored PRNG, while `OsRng`, `ThreadRng`, `RandomError`, `os_rng()`, `thread_rng()`, and `random_error()` are exported through the stdlib catalog. [DONE] /goal Add runtime-backed `core:sync/atomic.AtomicPtr<T>`. — DONE: added `AtomicPtr<T>` to `core:sync/atomic` as an owned `@RefCounted` native atomic raw-pointer cell with deterministic `Drop`, shared handle cloning/capture semantics, target-backed typed `load`, `store`, `swap`, and `compare_exchange` operations, and the same operation/order validation used by the integer and bool atomic wrappers. This completes the currently documented atomic handle family. [DONE] /goal Add live `std:process.Child` handles. — DONE: added runtime-backed live `Child` handles to `std:process`. Corrected async-contract follow-up superseded the original ordinary process-control shape: live `Command.spawn()` returns `Future<Child | IoError>`, `Child.wait()` returns `Future<ExitStatus | IoError>`, and `Child.kill()` returns `Future<null | IoError>` over private async runtime process-control machinery. `Child.id()` remains ordinary metadata; `Child.stdin()`, `stdout()`, and `stderr()` remain immediate `null | AsyncWriter/AsyncReader` accessors for the currently null streamed-child-stdio surface. The runtime stores live children in a handle table, releases the table entry when the last Otter handle drops, avoids holding the child-table mutex while internal OS waits run, and does not implicitly kill the OS process on handle drop. Registered JIT symbols, added runtime tests for spawn/wait/repeated wait/release and kill/wait, added e2e coverage for successful spawn, cached wait, cloned-handle wait, missing/invalid command errors, and kill, expanded `std:process` near-empty-prelude coverage for `Child`, added LSP member-completion coverage, and updated docs/17, docs/18, docs/24, docs/29, ROADMAP, and goals. Also scoped the parser so the documented method name `spawn` is accepted in interface/extend method declarations while top-level `function spawn()` remains rejected because `spawn` is an expression keyword. [DONE] /goal Add formatter wrapping for long cast chains. — DONE: `compiler::fmt` now wraps long top-level postfix cast chains at `as`/`is` token boundaries, reusing the shared infix-chain renderer at the parser's cast tier before method/arithmetic/value-operator chain wrappers. The original chain slice was deliberately conservative: comment/string/interpolation guardrails applied, it split lines with at least two top-level cast keywords whose neighboring tokens looked like a value-or-type operand and a non-union cast target type, and it refused lines with top-level binary operators so mixed-precedence expressions such as `left as A + right as B` kept using the lower-precedence additive wrapper instead of being visually misrepresented as one cast chain. Single top-level cast expressions were completed by the later single-cast slice. The shared angle classifier now treats generic type-argument closes followed by `as`/`is` as generic closes, so cast targets such as `Box<Alpha, Beta> as Result<Gamma<Delta, Epsilon>, Zeta>` keep tight generic spacing and do not split nested generic targets. Added a cast continuation line-state so already-formatted `as Type` / `is Type` lines stay idempotently indented. Added compiler formatter coverage for plain cast ladders, generic cast targets with nested/combo generic closers, and a top-level additive negative guard. [DONE] /goal Add formatter wrapping for long interface/extend body member lists. — DONE: `compiler::fmt` now wraps long one-line `interface ... { function ...; function ...; }` and `extend ... { function ... { ... } function ... { ... } }` bodies into one member per line with the closing brace on its own line. Default methods and extend method bodies stay intact, including semicolons inside method bodies. Added formatter coverage for semicolon-terminated interface declarations, interface default methods with internal semicolons, and extend method implementations. Expanded CLI `fmt --check`/rewrite and LSP whole-document formatting fixtures with a long `interface Service { ... }` body. Updated docs/23, ROADMAP, and the active formatter goal so the remaining formatter work is broader wrapping beyond parenthesized/bracketed/type-led brace/generic-angle/named-import brace/map-literal brace/match-arm brace/record-declaration brace comma lists/type-alias union pipe lists/function-return union pipe lists/interface-or-extend bound plus lists/interface-or-extend body member lists. [DONE] /goal Fix formatter spacing regressions around generic type arguments and async closures. — DONE: `compiler::fmt` no longer expands generic type arguments such as `Future<null>`, `JoinHandle<i64>`, or match-pattern constructors like `Joined<i64> j` into spaced comparison-looking forms, and it preserves the required space in async closure/function syntax such as `() async =>`. The fix recognizes `null` as a type argument in generic-angle classification, treats `async` as a valid token after a generic return type, uses a narrow text-aware type-name heuristic for generic constructor patterns during spacing normalization, explicitly separates `)`/`}` from following `async`, and stops same-line named-import closing paths from leaking continuation indentation into following declarations. Added focused compiler formatter, CLI `fmt --check`/rewrite, and LSP formatting regressions using the broken `Task.spawn(()async=>...)` / `Future < null >` shape plus an idempotency regression for `} from "path";` named imports. [DONE] /goal Thin empty-struct payload allocation where semantics permit it. — DONE: ordinary final structs with no runtime fields, no `Drop`, no `@RefCounted`, no transparent representation, and no extern layout now use a null field-block sentinel instead of allocating an empty managed payload. Union/dynamic boxes still carry semantic type ids, and interface objects still carry vtable/type ids, preserving `is`/`as`, pattern matching, dynamic dispatch, and GC observability. [DONE] /goal Add escape-analysis stack allocation for scalar final struct locals. — DONE: HIR codegen pre-scans function bodies for record- and tuple-struct locals whose binding-site literal/constructor is proven not to escape, allowing eligible ordinary final scalar-field structs to use zeroed Cranelift stack slots with the same in-frame field-block pointer representation. Whole-value escape uses such as calls, returns, aliases, address-of, casts, boxing, closure capture, and async capture disqualify the value; traced pointers, `@RefCounted`, channel endpoints, `Drop`, spread, transparent representation, arrays, and nested aggregate fields stay on the managed heap path. ROADMAP records CLIF/runtime regressions, JIT/native parity coverage, and `examples/stack_struct_bench.otter` benchmark coverage.

[DONE] /goal Add conservative scalar helper inlining. — DONE: backend codegen collects whole-HIR direct-call counts and uses them as a call-graph signal for a tiny direct-call inliner. Non-generic free functions over unmanaged scalar parameter/return types can be emitted inline when called once or trivially tiny, including simple scalar temporaries before a trailing expression; larger helpers called multiple times remain real calls to avoid code growth. Managed values, ownership-sensitive types, async, closures, assignments, control flow, nested calls, and allocations are deliberately excluded. ROADMAP records CLIF/runtime regressions, JIT/native parity coverage, and `examples/inlining_bench.otter` benchmark coverage.

[DONE] /goal Add staged interface receiver devirtualization. — DONE: HIR codegen recognizes interface method calls whose receiver is an immediately-created interface object, or a straight-line interface-typed local initialized/assigned from a known concrete implementor, and emits a direct monomorphized concrete impl call instead of allocating the interface wrapper and issuing `call_indirect`. Receiver evaluation is still rooted across argument evaluation for GC safety; facts survive same-concrete `if`/`match` joins and are dropped for captured locals, branch/loop/match-body assignments, mixed-concrete joins, interface parameters, and genuinely dynamic receivers. ROADMAP records CLIF/runtime backend regressions and `examples/interface_devirt_bench.otter` benchmark coverage.

[DONE] /goal Enable the production-safe backend optimization pass. — DONE: release builds ask Cranelift for its speed-oriented backend optimization pipeline while debug keeps CLIF readable, and CLI/native codegen is root-seeded from `main` or the exact test/bench body. Reachable callees, closures, async jobs, vtables, generic instances, and finalizers are pulled lazily so unused helpers and untouched stdlib functions are not emitted; allocation descriptors declare non-generic `Drop` finalizers on demand so dead-code trimming cannot remove finalizers reachable only from object headers. ROADMAP records backend JIT and native object-symbol regressions plus CLI debug/release smoke coverage.

[DONE] /goal Add connected UDP datagram operations to `std:net`. — DONE: extended the mixed provider-backed `UdpSocket` handle with connected UDP datagram operations. Corrected async-contract follow-up superseded the original ordinary-result shape: live `connect`, `peer_addr`, `send`, `recv`, `send_to`, `recv_from`, setup/control, options, multicast, and close methods all return futures where they can touch provider state. Runtime hooks use provider connected-UDP filtering/default-peer behavior, preserve arbitrary bytes through the existing hex ABI, append received bytes into caller-owned `Bytes`, and map provider failures into `IoError` values behind async public contracts. [DONE] /goal Add timed TCP connect to `std:net`. — DONE: extended `TcpStream` with `connect_timeout(addr, timeout: Duration)`, backed by the provider TCP connect-with-timeout operation. Historical note: this originally returned `TcpStream | IoError`; the corrected async-contract slice later superseded the public shape to `Future<TcpStream | IoError>` and removed the exported direct runtime ABI from code generation. The runtime validates the public duration as non-negative nanoseconds, maps invalid/failed connects into `IoError` behind the async public contract, and registers successful streams in the same deterministic handle registry as `connect`.

[DONE] /goal Add TCP stream peek to `std:net`. — DONE at the time: extended `TcpStream` with stream peek, backed by the provider non-consuming stream peek operation. Later corrected-contract work superseded this public shape: live `TcpStream.peek(buf)` returns `Future<i64 | IoError>` and must be awaited. The stdlib method follows the same caller-owned `Bytes` convention as stream reads, appending peeked bytes to the supplied buffer and returning the byte count while leaving the stream readable. Runtime preserves arbitrary byte payloads through the existing hex ABI and reports invalid negative lengths as `IoError` behind the async public contract. Expanded runtime peek-before-read coverage, TCP loopback e2e coverage, LSP analysis and member-completion coverage, native parity, and updated docs/18, docs/24, ROADMAP, and goals while keeping deeper readiness-native provider integration, IDNA, and additional platform-specific socket options planned.

The stdlib methods follow the same caller-owned `Bytes` convention as `recv`/`recv_from`, appending peeked bytes while leaving the datagram available for a later receive. Runtime preserves arbitrary byte payloads through the existing hex/length-framed ABI and reports invalid negative lengths as `IoError` behind async public contracts. ---

general:

/goal Analyze the current state of the project, go thought each doc, read all the necessary source code, run tests if needed. Then, update roadmap, ensure roadmap is up to date. Be detailed. We will use the roadmap to complete goals in goals.txt.

/goal Finish Otter Fusion end to end as a production-ready language, using ROADMAP.md and the docs as the authoritative backlog. Complete every remaining core language, compiler, runtime, backend, standard-library, package-manager, tooling, LSP/editor, documentation, example, and test-suite item recorded in the roadmap or design docs, one test-gated slice at a time. This includes closing the advanced deferrals that still block a truly complete toolchain: deeper backend/MIR-or-equivalent optimization work, any remaining std/core provider and standard-library surfaces, package-manager deferred features, LSP follow-ups, formatter/comment-preservation work, GC throughput work that remains behavior-neutral, and roadmap Phase 7 embedding-engine work unless explicitly moved out of scope by a design decision. Follow AGENTS.md and the docs; do not take shortcuts or silently change language design. Preserve clean layering, modular architecture, semantics, GC/rooting/finalizer safety, async/task/channel correctness, deterministic `@RefCounted` behavior, observability (`otter_fusion emit tokens|ast|hir|clif`, DWARF, `run --time`/`exec --time`), JIT/native parity, examples, docs, and the VS Code/LSP experience. Every completed slice must include focused unit/integration/e2e tests, negative/regression coverage where relevant, benchmark or timing coverage for performance-sensitive work, docs/examples/ROADMAP updates, and goals.txt bookkeeping. This umbrella goal is not done until ROADMAP.md has no remaining unimplemented core feature, advanced deferral, documented surface, or required quality gate except items explicitly and intentionally deferred beyond the production-ready language scope.

/goal UNFUCK the async/blocking contract across Otter Fusion under the corrected rule: Otter Fusion does not support any public blocking API. Nuke, remove, destroy, kill, and annihilate every public function, method, example, doc, LSP signature, test expectation, or roadmap claim that exposes blocking behavior. Anything that would wait on IO, process execution, filesystem, networking, timers, channels, locks, child processes, stdin/stdout/stderr, DNS, sockets, files, or OS/thread coordination must be async/non-blocking at the Otter Fusion language surface. No "clearly blocking" public escape hatches, no "dedicated blocking thread" public API story, no documented intentional public blocking boundary, no copied Rust blocking convention, and no ordinary sync function that secretly waits. Internal host/runtime waits may exist only as private implementation machinery behind async/reactor-backed futures or other non-blocking public contracts, with GC native/no-root state correctly bracketed, cancellation cleanup, reactor wakeups, timeout behavior, rooting/finalizer safety, JIT/native parity, LSP/editor parity, examples, docs, and stress/regression tests. Audit and replace the currently suspect surfaces first: std:net synchronous TcpStream/TcpListener/UdpSocket operations and DNS, std:process status/output/spawn/wait/kill and environment hooks, std:fs/std:io sync helpers and handles, channel iterator blocking recv, Thread.spawn as a public blocking-work recommendation, std:time.sleep, runtime GC blocking-call brackets, docs/20, docs/21, docs/24, examples, tests, and ROADMAP wording. Otter Fusion is its own 100% async language, not Rust with different syntax: use Rust only as occasional engineering inspiration, never as default API shape, naming, ownership tradeoff, blocking convention, or ecosystem template. Do not silently change language design; ask before removing or renaming public APIs, but the target design is non-negotiable: 100% of public IO and wait-capable operations are async/non-blocking. Every slice must include focused unit/integration/e2e tests, negative/regression coverage, direct examples, docs/ROADMAP/goals updates, and process-clean verification. Do not leave cargo/rustc/test/otter processes running or consuming CPU; after every slice and after any interruption, scan for and terminate stray CPU-heavy processes.

optimizations:

/goal Push Otter Fusion from the current production-safe backend optimization pass toward a deeper optimizing compiler. Design and implement a MIR/SSA-oriented optimization pipeline or equivalent backend optimization layer, staged and test-gated, with interprocedural escape analysis, broader inlining with real cost modeling and recursion/code-growth guards, constant folding and propagation, dead-store/dead-branch elimination, loop-aware optimizations where safe, richer devirtualization/monomorphization of statically-known dynamic/interface paths, target-aware lowering improvements, and tighter stdlib/package code emission where it fits the existing module/provider architecture. Preserve language semantics, GC/rooting/finalizer safety, `@RefCounted` deterministic release behavior, async/task/channel correctness, and all observability (`otter_fusion emit tokens|ast|hir|clif`, DWARF, `run --time`/`exec --time`). Prove JIT/native parity and optimization soundness with focused backend unit tests, CLI integration tests, e2e cases, negative/regression tests for safety boundaries, GC-stress/concurrency cases where relevant, and benchmark coverage for every optimization slice. Keep docs, ROADMAP, examples, and goals consistent.

new features:

/goal Design and build the full standard library as production infrastructure, not a bag of compiler magic. Stage it module-by-module according to docs/18 and docs/24: keep `core:*` compiler/runtime substrate and language-privileged utilities separate from `std:*` official library APIs and target-backed implementations; allow modules to be authored in Otter Fusion, Rust-backed runtime/compiler code, or mixed layers; route `core:*`/`std:*` availability through the stdlib catalog/provider architecture so future target/custom sysroots can replace, omit, or extend `std:*` packages without changing source-level import syntax; progressively move toolchain library definitions out of the monolithic embedded `PRELUDE_SRC` into maintainable std/core source modules or an equivalent well-structured toolchain package; keep the near-empty prelude rule; implement each module with unit, integration, and e2e tests, docs, examples, and LSP support. Current priority slices per ROADMAP.md and docs/18/docs/24: named-zone timezone database/conversion extensions and leap-second policy for `std:time`; deeper readiness-native provider integration, richer async network timeout ergonomics after an Otter-specific API decision, stress/performance coverage, and additional platform-specific socket options for `std:net`; streamed child stdio for `std:process`; cryptographic-strength APIs and remaining continuous distribution helpers for `std:rand`; pinned `Bytes`/`Buffer` views for `std:bytes`; collection follow-ups such as set literal syntax, hash-backed `Set`, and keyed collection construction; provider/catalog/sysroot hardening; and any later documented std/core surfaces that are not already implemented. Do not change language design without asking.



/goal Implement the embedding engine `std:engine` (docs/26, ROADMAP Phase 7): run guest Otter Fusion inside a sandboxed isolate. Follow docs/26 exactly and the locked design decisions — bridged data crosses by COPY-BY-VALUE over a `@Bridge` repr-C layout (never a shared heap pointer), and resource isolation uses PER-ISOLATE runtime state (each isolate owns its heap/GC/registries, selected by a thread-local current-isolate pointer). Build on the proven macro-host substrate (crates/macros + crates/backend/macro_host). Production quality, no shortcuts; ask first before any language-design change. Stage it per ROADMAP Phase 7 (each step shippable): (1) single-isolate load/invoke returning primitives; (2) compile-time capability-whitelist enforcement of std:/core:/pkg:/file:; (3) the `@Bridge` ABI (frozen repr-C layout, bridge-compatibility checking, field-by-field copy marshalling, str-as-bytes); (4) host bindings into virtual `host:` modules + engine-managed bridge channels/Shared spanning the boundary + Unit entry introspection; (5) per-isolate runtime state threaded through every lang_* entry point with per-isolate stop-the-world GC; (6) hard limits (max_heap_bytes/max_alloc_bytes/timeout_ms/max_stack_depth via per-isolate accounting + deadline/cancel flag at existing safepoints) + Stats; (7) async entries polled on the isolate executor from the host's await (no user-visible block_on); (8) cold-start caching with a content-addressed compiled-artifact store over the native-object path plus a warm isolate pool, with cache keys covering source, policy, @Bridge layouts, target triple, and compiler/runtime version and with heap snapshots explicitly out of scope unless docs/26 changes. Do many tests — unit + integration + e2e: capability denial as a compile error, @Bridge round-trips for every bridge type (host↔guest), host bindings + bridge channels, arbitrary-entry invocation by name, per-isolate heap independence, hard memory cap → OutOfMemory, timeout → Timeout (incl. tight-loop latency), guest-panic containment → GuestPanic, async entry awaited by a host, cold-cache miss and warm-cache hit correctness, policy/ABI cache-key mismatch rejection, and warm-isolate heap/registry independence; JIT + native parity + GC-stress. Keep docs/26, examples, LSP and ROADMAP consistent and up to date.
